# Findings Log

Append-only. One line per finding: `date · type · state · gist`. Full rules in
[`PROJECT.md`](../PROJECT.md) → *Findings-Log Discipline*; spec § *Factory model and SDLC* is the origin.

**Type — what kind of thing it is.** `bug` · `process` · `note` · `fact` · `risk` · `idea` ·
`optimization`. This is the **observed** vocabulary, not a new mandate: it is what the writers of this
log actually used. Kickoff declared four (`process | bug | idea | data`) and `data` was never once
used, so the artifact is the authority over the older statement.

**State — what happens to it next.** `closed` (resolved in the run that found it, or a lesson needing
no action) · `open` (live work) · `accepted` (a real limitation, decided against on the record).
Entries written before 2026-08-01 carry no state; the first triage pass assigns one.

**Only the HC promotes, and nothing urgent waits for that.**

> **No Issues, PRs, rules, or ADRs spawn directly from findings.** The HC promotes `open` findings to
> tracked work at a triage pass — the `do-now` disposition, and the only path from this file to an
> Issue. Separately and always: **if something is broken it is a defect**, fixed in the running change
> when the fix is in hand or tracked immediately when it is not. Filing that is correct, not a
> violation. What this rule governs is *learnings and proposals*, never things that are simply broken.

An agent that has just learned something process-shaped writes **one line** and continues. A finding
matching a class below records **a reference plus what is new about this instance**, not a
re-derivation of the lesson.

## Recurring classes

Descriptive index of shapes recorded more than once — **not rules**. An imperative statement belongs
in the Rules Layer, which nadal does not author. Line references are to this file as of `10e6189`
(`git show 10e6189:docs/findings.md`), since a header edit shifts every later line.

| Class | Shape | Seen |
|---|---|---|
| **C1** | Fixed the named instance, not the class | ~20 · L77 L132 L185 L215 L248 |
| **C2** | A claim outruns what the code enforces — comment, test title, doc, PR body, quantifier | ~17 · L93 L214 L246 L261 |
| **C3** | A review round's fix creates the next round's defect | ~9 · L155 L192 L209 L256 |
| **C4** | Testing only the configuration the test invented | ~8 · L142 L157 L197 L238 |
| **C5** | An enumeration fails silently at its edges — derive the guard from structure | ~7 · L87 L114 L190 L237 |
| **C6** | A behavioral merge hazard with no textual signature | 5 · L180 L231 L239 |

## Triage watermark

What the last pass covered, so the next one reads only what is new. This replaces a per-entry state
field: a promoted finding **is** its Issue, a residual **is** its closed Issue, and everything else is
archive by default — so the only thing that has to be durable is the boundary.

| Pass | Date | Covered | Promoted | Register |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2026-08-01 | every entry through `10e6189`, swept for open items | #62 #63 #64 #65 #66 | #67–#77 |

**Next pass reads entries appended after `10e6189` only.** The register is the do-not-re-litigate
store: check it before raising anything that sounds familiar —
`gh issue list --state closed --label residual`.

- 2026-07-30 · process · merge-autonomy on green+attestation structurally races the SDD scoped re-review; holds must be stated in the dispatch, not mid-flight

- 2026-07-30 · idea · tn's summary-line safety (control-char strip, bidi strip, quote/backslash escaping, field-injection resistance) was derived under adversarial review for one command; it is a CLI-wide contract worth a single shared module + one test suite
- 2026-07-30 · process · the CLI-wide sanitize/summary contract logged directly above already landed in-plan during Task 7 — `src/sanitize.ts` + `src/cli/summary.ts`, covered by `test/sanitize.test.ts` + `test/summary.test.ts`; recorded so triage does not re-open it
- 2026-07-30 · process · `enforce-clean-tree.sh` blocks the *whole* multi-line Bash tool call when any single segment is a destructive git op on a dirty tree, not just that segment — an earlier harmless command (e.g. `rm -f`) chained before the blocked `git checkout --` in the same call silently never runs either; split destructive git ops into their own Bash call to avoid losing sibling commands.
- 2026-07-30 · process · a regenerate-and-diff drift guard must not use `git diff --exit-code <dir>`: git diff only inspects TRACKED files, so a brand-new untracked artifact (the most likely drift output, e.g. a fresh drizzle migration .sql + snapshot) passes silently; check `git status --porcelain <dir>` instead, and prove the guard red-green by inducing real drift
- 2026-07-30 · process · this plan's CI task specified a `parity` job that the vendored ace baseline already provided in `.github/workflows/parity.yml`; plan authors must inventory vendored workflows before specifying CI, and a Host App must never re-implement a vendored check it is forbidden to edit
- 2026-07-30 · process · `docs/findings.md` was mandated from Task 1 onward by a Global Constraint but only created by Task 9, so early tasks improvised a non-spec line format (`- date - kind - text` instead of the spec's `date · type · gist`); a plan must create an append-target before the first task required to append to it
- 2026-07-30 · process · `gh api -f` sends numeric fields as JSON strings — the issue-dependencies endpoint 422s on `-f issue_id=<n>` ("not of type integer"); use `-F` (typed) for any integer/bool field
- 2026-07-30 · process · GitHub's native issue-dependencies REST API (`issues/<n>/dependencies/blocked_by`, keyed on numeric issue id) is confirmed live and scriptable on this repo; future plan text can drop the "may need a prose fallback" hedge
- 2026-07-30 · process · surfacing a previously-swallowed error (telemetry write failures, this wave's item 5) can double stderr output when the underlying failure is shared — an ENOTDIR TN_DB_PATH fails both the command's own summary write and telemetry's own openDb() at the same broken path — so a fix-round dispatch naming only one test file to update should be read as "at least," not "only": grep the whole suite for assertions the new code path could now trip
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (deferred, Medium, Codex round-1 on PR #20) test/cli-grammar-parity.test.ts's strengthened checks still accept a duplicate GRAMMAR.md row for the same command — Set-membership + a Map keyed by noun/verb both collapse duplicates, so a second identical `tn db migrate` row would pass every check, missing the documented "one spelling per operation" invariant
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (deferred, Low, Codex round-1 on PR #20) docs/cli/GRAMMAR.md's new summary-line paragraph (item 9) states every value field is double-quoted, but its own worked example and the actual emitted `status=ok`/`status=error` field are unquoted — scope the claim to interpolated/free-form values (path=, message=), or quote status too and update db-migrate.ts to match
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (deferred, Low, Codex round-2 on PR #20) src/telemetry/request-log.ts's `sanitizeValue(err.message)` assumes `.message` is always a string — a mutated/custom Error with a non-string `message` would make `.replace()` throw inside the catch, violating "telemetry must never break the request itself"; harden with a `String(...)` coercion before sanitizing
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (deferred, Low, Codex round-2 on PR #20) test/db-membership-unique-upgrade.test.ts asserts only that one row survives the dedup DELETE, not that it's specifically the lowest-id row the migration comment promises to keep — tighten the assertion to check the surviving row's id
- 2026-07-30 · process · a codex:rescue Agent call's job record can land under the CALLING session's own workspace-hash directory (here `dynamic-foraging-hollerith-*`, the driver's own worktree) rather than the target repo's (`wild-floating-firefly-*`) even though `workspaceRoot` inside the JSON correctly names the calling session's cwd — when hunting for a job record, glob `~/.claude/plugins/data/codex-openai-codex/state/*/jobs/*.json` across ALL workspace-hash dirs, not just the one matching the repo under review
- 2026-07-30 · process · a rule recorded only as prose the checker cannot parse is not a rule — nadal's merge-autonomy intent lived in a PROJECT.md subsection honestly headed "recorded, not machine-enforced" while every binding surface said a human merges, so agents read the binding surfaces, the parity check read only the value, and ten PRs merged against the written rule with a green build the whole time; fixed upstream by ace ADR 0037, which makes any such heading a parity-check error
- 2026-07-30 · process · governance and harness permission are two independent layers and fixing one does nothing for the other — a repo may set `merge: attested`, satisfy every gate condition, and still be unable to merge because the agent runtime denies the command; conversely a permission grant does not authorize a merge the config forbids
- 2026-07-30 · process · under `defaultMode: auto` the classifier can supersede a matching `permissions.allow` rule — `Bash(gh pr:*)` was already allowed globally and `gh pr merge` was still refused, because the classifier soft-denies destructive/irreversible actions independently of the allow list; the targeted fix is an `autoMode.allow` entry, not another `permissions.allow` entry
- 2026-07-30 · process · a subagent's deliverable must be a FILE, not its final message — 7 idle-without-delivering occurrences across 3 sessions, 3 model tiers, and both implementer and reviewer roles, and in every single case the work had been done and only the message was lost; switching to "write the report via heredoc, then reply with one line" produced both reports immediately
- 2026-07-30 · process · verify a subagent's report file EXISTS (`test -f` plus a grep for the covering-test evidence) before dispatching the re-review that depends on it — a fix report claimed in a status contract was never actually written, and the re-reviewer was dispatched with a dangling input that failed silently at dispatch time
- 2026-07-30 · process · re-vendor ace by copying ONLY the files the upstream change touched, not by re-running `bin/ace-sync` — the sync script's ALLOW list includes `.claude` and would overwrite nadal's own settings.json (hooks + model) since only PROJECT.md and bin/setup are preserved surfaces
- 2026-07-30 · process · ace's new `check_unenforced_headings` ratchet caught its first real target on the very commit that introduced it — restoring the "recorded, not machine-enforced" heading in a scratch copy failed parity at PROJECT.md:373, so the guard was proven red-before-green against the exact defect it was written for
- 2026-07-30 · process · `autoMode` is IGNORED in a committed project `.claude/settings.json` AND in `.claude/settings.local.json` — verified with a marker rule from a fresh invocation in a throwaway project; only `~/.claude/settings.json` (user) and `--settings <file>` (invocation) are honored, so the classifier permission that lets an agent run `gh pr merge` cannot be repo-tracked and must live in user or invocation scope
- 2026-07-30 · process · a committed `autoMode` block reads as authoritative and binds nothing — the same defect class as the merge-gate prose it was meant to complete, one layer down; caught by the first PR ever to run the `attested` gate (Codex [high], driver-confirmed), which is why the gate refused the merge
- 2026-07-30 · process · wave item 10 only PARTLY closed the temp-dir leak: the new test/helpers/tn-db.ts covers the four files it migrated, but ~15 `mkdtempSync` sites remain outside it (db-migrate, db-membership-unique*, cli-db-migrate-cwd, cli-main-exit-code, db-client*, plus two mid-test overrides in cli-db-migrate-command) — the helper exists, so finishing the migration is mechanical
- 2026-07-30 · fact · TennisLink is no longer a public source — every `tennislink.usta.com` leagues/ and tournaments/ path (StatsAndStandings, TeamProfile, Search, Scorecard, EventInfo) 302s to `account.usta.com/authorize?...&audience=tennislink`, and USTA's own support docs now say stats and standings require a signed-in account; spec § Ingestion lists TennisLink as ingestion path 1 ("public") and it belongs on path 2 (login-assisted, headed Chrome), which the spec already provides — no architecture change, but no TennisLink fixture can be captured without an HC login session
- 2026-07-30 · fact · TennisRecord's `matchhistory.aspx` is public and carries the entire scouting substrate the TennisLink player page was wanted for — per court: date, league (18+/40+), team, court slot (S1/D1-D4), partner + dynamic rating, opponents + dynamic ratings, W/L, set scores including match-tiebreak notation, per-match rating, resulting dynamic rating, and a `mid=` match id; court-slot tendencies, partner frequency, prior meetings and windowed records are all derivable from it, so the TennisLink login gate delays corroboration rather than blocking Phase 3
- 2026-07-30 · fact · WTN is not a separate fetch — the ITF widget (`.v-form-wtn-widget`) is embedded in the USTA player-search profile page, and carries confidence band and game zone in addition to the singles/doubles numbers; spec § Ingestion lists WTN as its own path, which is true organisationally and not operationally: one fetch, two parsers
- 2026-07-30 · risk · nadal is a PUBLIC repository and the source pages are about real people — the USTA profile page carries a `nameGenderAddress` block with name, gender and city/state — so fixtures cut from real captures are redacted through `tools/redact-fixture.ts` and the substitution map is deliberately kept outside the repo (committing it would publish exactly what redaction removes)
- 2026-07-30 · bug · a whole-string spelling list (literal / percent-encoded / plus-encoded) is not enough to find an identity in captured HTML: TennisRecord emits `teamname=Gerleman%2c Garrett` — comma encoded, space literal — which matches none of the three, and nine real surnames survived a redaction that reported success; fixed by matching per character (each non-alphanumeric may be literal or percent-encoded, space may also be `+`) and caught only because the structural detector sweep re-derives identities from the page's own markup rather than trusting the list
- 2026-07-30 · bug · `div.large table` is ambiguous on a TennisRecord team page — it holds both the roster and the local schedule — so a container-scoped selector silently parses schedule rows as roster entries; anchoring on a content marker (the table with an `NTRP` column, within `div.large`) is both narrower and more durable, and the mutation test that removed the first `div.large` is what exposed it
- 2026-07-30 · fact · TennisRecord renders every list twice, `div.large` for desktop and `div.small` for mobile, and the two are NOT interchangeable — the desktop match-history table has the full per-court detail but no opponent-team column while the mobile block names the opponent team, so the parser reads the desktop table and correlates the opponent team by position, verifying the per-row dates agree rather than trusting the ordering
- 2026-07-30 · process · a regex over `<tr style='...'>` missed the last row of a 14-row table (its style attribute differs), which would have shipped a parser that silently drops one match per page — the same page parsed with a DOM selector returned all 14; row-splitting by regex on real HTML is the defect, not the fixture
- 2026-07-30 · note · deferred to Phase 3 (#15): `court_matches` has no source-match-id column, but the match-history parser recovers one from the result link (`matchresults.aspx?...&mid=`) — that id is what makes a re-pull idempotent at court granularity (spec § Ingestion's first discipline), so #15 should add the column rather than dedupe on a composite key
- 2026-07-30 · note · deferred to Phase 3/4: a TennisRecord team page also carries the team's local schedule (date, time, opponent, site, result) in a second `div.large` block — team-match level data the Phase 2 scope did not include, and a cheaper path to `team_matches` rows than any TennisLink page
- 2026-07-30 · process · `skills/ship/SKILL.md` still asserts merge is `required` always ("a run that merged its own PR is a failed run regardless of the setting") in its quality-gate section, contradicting its own `<what-to-do>` and `<procedure>` sections which describe `attested`; ace ADR 0037 updated the latter and missed the former — an upstream fix, not a nadal edit, since `skills/` is vendored
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex adversarial review, PR #26, rated critical) a per-character encoding matcher that knows only literal / percent / plus spellings still misses HTML character references — a server may write any character as `&#67;` or `&#x43;`, and `O&#39;Brien` is an ordinary way to emit an apostrophe in a name — so an identity could survive both substitution and the forbidden sweep of a PUBLIC-repo privacy control; fixed by adding decimal and hex entity alternatives per character AND sweeping an entity-decoded copy during verification, so an encoding the tool cannot yet substitute still fails the capture rather than shipping
- 2026-07-30 · bug · TennisRecord prints match scores WINNER-first, not player-first — every `L` row shows the opponent winning both sets (`6-3 6-3` on a loss) — so the original `games: [number, number]` tuple would have been read as player-first and inverted games-won for every loss in every dossier; the emitted type now names the fields `winnerGames`/`loserGames` so the orientation cannot be misread, and is proven from the fixture rather than assumed
- 2026-07-30 · process · a reviewer finding can be right about the defect and wrong about the fix — Codex flagged the match-tiebreak rule via a comment of mine that mislabelled `6-7 3-1 1-0` as a retirement, and recommended throwing on ambiguous notation; investigating the actual fixture showed the notation is consistent (winner-first, `1-0` marking the deciding-set tiebreak) and that the real defects were the wrong comment, a missing assertion on the real row, and the undocumented tuple orientation — take the substance, decline the remedy, and say which is which
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex, PR #26) three fail-open paths in the match-history parser, all of the same shape — a mobile block that stops linking an opponent team, an unrecognised score component, and a participant cell missing a player — each produced a record that looked complete while quietly missing data; all three now throw, and the participant check enforces cardinality by discipline (doubles: partner + 2 opponents; singles: 1 opponent) except on an explicit default
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex, PR #26) the capture tool redacted the provenance `sourceUrl` but never verified it, so a URL-only identity absent from the substitution map would have been written to a public provenance file — a second publication surface with the same exposure as the fixture, guarded only by the operator's memory; it now goes through the same redact-then-verify call as the page
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 2, PR #26) "winner-first" is right about the source and wrong as a field name — TennisRecord orients each set to the MATCH winner, who still loses a set in any three-setter, so `winnerGames`/`loserGames` reads as *set* winner and is contradicted by the fixture's own `4-6 7-5 1-0` loss row; renamed to `matchWinnerGames`/`matchLoserGames` and asserted against both a straight-sets loss and the mixed-set one, since a test that only checks the straight-sets row passes for the wrong reason
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 2) throwing on an unrecognised score component does not cover an EMPTY score cell — `lines()` returns `[]` and the loop never runs, so a stripped Result cell yielded a W/L record with `sets: []`, indistinguishable downstream from a legitimately scoreless match; the same shape applied to an emptied league-context or team cell, and all three now throw (defaults exempted from the score check, since nobody played)
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 2) an alignment walk that picks the first alternative by `startsWith` desynchronises whenever a character's encoding BEGINS with the character — `&` is a prefix of `&#38;`, `%` of `%25` — consuming one character of a five-character sequence and corrupting every subsequent offset; fixed by ordering alternatives longest-first, which matters here because real team names contain `&` (`HOA/Thyagarajan/18&over4.0M`)
- 2026-07-30 · note · the `mid` query parameter on a TennisRecord result link is now a REQUIRED field rather than an optional one — it is the court-level idempotency key Phase 3 (#15) needs, so a record without it looks usable and cannot be reconciled on the next pull; if a legitimate page is ever found without one, model that state explicitly rather than relaxing the check
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 3, PR #26) a "both are null" guard cannot see PARTIAL drift — renaming only the WTN SINGLES title left DOUBLES parsing fine, so the top-level guard stayed quiet and a real singles rating was silently erased into a result indistinguishable from a doubles-only player; every widget section is now inspected individually, and a titled section with a value but no recognised title throws
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 3) `Number("")` is `0` and therefore FINITE, so a `Number.isFinite(Number(raw))` guard accepts an emptied cell as a rating of zero — the empty case has to be tested explicitly; caught by the very test written for the finding above, which is why the mutation test earns its keep over an eyeball review
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 3) anchoring a positionally-decoded table on ONE header proves the right table was found and nothing about where anything sits inside it — remove the roster's Location column and every later field shifts by one while the nullable parsers absorb the mismatch into plausible nulls; the full ordered column contract is now asserted before any positional decoding, for both the team roster and the season-aggregate table
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 3) `?? 0` on an aggregate cell converts column drift into STATISTICS — a season row of zero wins, zero losses, zero games reads as a player who did not play and is indistinguishable from one who did; aggregate cells now require a real number
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 3) a structural sweep must cover what a human reading the page would recognise, not only what looks like an identifier — the USTA detectors checked uaid and tennis-id but not the displayed name, so a map containing the uaid and omitting the name replaced the id, passed both sweeps, and shipped the real name; detectors added for the displayed name and location, verified by confirming an unlisted name is caught
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 3) a redaction stand-in containing `'` or `"` would terminate the attribute it lands in and corrupt the fixture's markup; rejecting unsafe replacement values at the input boundary is stricter than escaping and, unlike parsing and re-serialising the document, does not alter markup by itself — `&` stays legal because real team names contain it
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 4, PR #26) a MINIMUM cell count does not protect positional decoding — insert one numeric column mid-table and the row still clears the minimum while every offset after it shifts, so totals parse "successfully" from the wrong columns; the season-aggregate table now asserts an exact ordered header contract and an exact body-cell count, matching the roster's
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 4) validating each recognised section independently still accepts DUPLICATES — `find` takes the first and drops the rest, so a responsive or stale second WTN widget would silently decide which rating a dossier shows; the USTA page already renders its identity block twice (desktop + mobile variants), so this is not hypothetical markup
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 4) the terminating semicolon on an HTML numeric character reference is OPTIONAL — browsers recover `&#67ory Hogan` and render it as `Cory Hogan` — so a redaction matcher and decoder that require it can be walked past by markup that displays perfectly; both now accept the semicolon-less form, with a negative lookahead so `&#67` cannot match inside a longer reference like `&#671;`
- 2026-07-30 · process · four adversarial review rounds on one PR found 18 real defects and ZERO false positives among the throw sites, including on a round explicitly prompted to hunt for over-strict guards (a bye, a walkover, an unrated player, a mixed league) — the finding count fell 5, 4, 6, 3 across rounds while the defect class stayed constant (a structural page change yielding data that looks complete), which is the signal that the class was systemic rather than incidental
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 5, PR #26) the ordered-column contract had been applied to two positionally-decoded tables and missed on two others — the recent-team table and, most importantly, the match-history desktop table itself, the highest-value table in the project; fixing a defect class table-by-table leaves the ones nobody happened to look at, so the check now lives in one shared `assertColumns` helper that every positional decoder routes through
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 5) guarding duplicate WTN section titles inside the FIRST widget says nothing about a second widget — `.first()` would silently pick one by DOM order and drop a conflicting rating; the same reasoning as the section guard, one level up, which is the tell that a guard was written for an instance rather than for the class
- 2026-07-30 · process · five adversarial rounds, 20 real defects, zero false positives — and rounds 4 and 5 each found the PREVIOUS round's fix applied too narrowly (a minimum where an exact count was needed; a duplicate guard one nesting level too deep; a contract on two tables out of four). The recurring lesson is not the individual defects but that a fix aimed at the reported instance rather than the class reliably leaves siblings behind, and an independent reviewer finds them in the next pass
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 6, PR #26, merge-blocking) header count is NOT row width — the roster's `Rating` header carries `colspan="2"`, so 8 headers describe 9 body cells, and a guard written as `cells.length < headers.length` accepted a row with an extra cell inserted before the rating and read a different column as the rating; the PR text claimed an "exact per-row cell count" which was true for three of the four tables and false for this one, so the claim outran the code
- 2026-07-30 · process · (Codex round 6) the roster's column-contract tests passed for a reason other than their titles claimed — they mutate HEADERS, so `assertColumns` throws before the row-width guard is ever reached, leaving the body-decode path untested; a mutation that leaves the header alone and shifts only the body is the test that actually exercises it, and this is the concrete form of `rules/testing.md`'s "would I know if this passed but the feature were broken?"
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 6) a redaction replacement may not INTRODUCE a URL-structural character (`& = ? #`) the original lacked — per-character encoding styles are recovered from the original, so a character present only in the replacement is emitted literally and `playername=A&B` splits the query; characters present on both sides are safe because their encoding was observed, which is why the earlier ampersand test passed without covering this
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 7, PR #26, merge-blocking) `.filter(row => row.name !== "")` after a positional decode is a FAIL-OPEN in disguise — it looks like defensive tidying and it silently deletes a player from a roster, an affiliation from a profile, or a season from an aggregate table while every other field still looks complete; found in three parsers by the reviewer and in a fourth (the season-year drop) by applying the finding to its siblings, all four now throw
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 7) a null-only guard on a query parameter misses the EMPTY value — `hrefParam` returns `""` for `mid=`, so the court-level idempotency key could ship blank while the guard directly above it claimed it was required; the existing test removed the parameter entirely and therefore never exercised the case
- 2026-07-30 · process · seven adversarial rounds, 25 real defects, zero false positives, and the class narrowed rather than the count falling monotonically (5, 4, 6, 3, 2, 2, 3) — rounds 6 and 7 both found defects whose tests EXISTED and passed for a different reason than their titles claimed, which is the single highest-yield question to ask of a test suite and is worth making a standing review prompt rather than an occasional one
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 8, PR #26, critical, merge-blocking) a hand-written named-entity table is the WRONG SHAPE for a privacy control — HTML5 defines over two thousand named references, and `Cory&NewLine;Hogan` renders as the name while evading any list that does not happen to include `&NewLine;` or `&Tab;`; the fix is not a longer table but a standards-complete decoder, so verification now also sweeps a rendered view built by parsing the document (text nodes AND attribute values, whitespace collapsed), which is complete by construction and stays complete as the spec moves
- 2026-07-30 · process · the durable lesson across eight rounds is about the SHAPE of a guard, not its contents — a hand-written enumeration (entity table, spelling list, single anchor column, null-only check) fails silently at its edges, while a guard derived from the structure itself (parse and read back, ordered column contract, exact row width, structural detector sweep) fails closed at them; every round-over-round regression on this PR was an enumeration that needed to become a derivation
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 9, PR #26, critical, merge-blocking) a rendered view built from `.text()` plus attributes is still incomplete — cheerio does not return COMMENT bodies, so `<!-- Cory&NewLine;Hogan -->` evaded all three sweeps while rendering as the identity; comment contents are raw text, so each is now parsed in turn and read back, giving it the same standards-complete decoding as the document
- 2026-07-30 · bug · a node walk that returns early on any non-`tag` node never leaves the DOCUMENT ROOT (its type is `root`, not `tag`), so it silently collects nothing — and an existing attribute test kept passing because a DIFFERENT sweep happened to catch that case, which is why the regression only surfaced when a test was added for a case only the walk could catch; recurse on anything with children, and treat a guard that passes because of a neighbouring mechanism as untested
- 2026-07-30 · process · nine adversarial rounds, 27 real defects, zero false positives; the last three findings were all in the same 40-line verification function, each one a completeness gap in a different node type (text -> attributes -> comments), which is the signature of a guard that should be derived from the document structure rather than assembled from the node kinds someone thought to list
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 10, PR #26, critical, merge-blocking) collapsing `\s+` does not normalise ZERO-WIDTH characters — U+200B is not JavaScript whitespace, so `&ZeroWidthSpace;` decoded into an identity leaves it matching neither the raw pattern nor the spaced one, while rendering as the name; the rendered verification view now maps the whole Cf category plus the zero-width set to a space before collapsing
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 10) including `\s` in a base64 payload character class made the match run past the end of an UNQUOTED attribute value and eat the separator before the next attribute — `<img src=data:...,QUJD alt=logo>` became `...REDACTEDalt=logo`, silently deleting `alt` from a tool whose entire purpose is preserving markup
- 2026-07-30 · process · (Codex round 10) a test named "leaves the markup structurally identical" compared only tag and class, so it passed while an attribute was being deleted — the name promised more than the assertion checked; it now compares attribute NAMES (catching deletion) plus class VALUES (what selectors read), deliberately not other attribute values, which redaction is supposed to change
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 10) a nullable correlation key silently downgrades the check that depends on it — the mobile block's date was parsed as optional and the desktop/mobile comparison skipped when it was null, leaving count-only positional matching under which a reorder misattributes every later opponent team, while the parser's own comment claimed position was verified by dates; the date is now required
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (Codex round 11, PR #26, critical, merge-blocking) Unicode normalisation is a redaction bypass — `José` composed (U+00E9) and decomposed (`e` + U+0301) are the same name to every reader and different code-point sequences to a matcher, so a substitution map written in one form silently misses the other and an accented real name ships; verification now folds every view AND every forbidden/allow-list value to NFC, and a percent-decoded view was added so the same identity hidden in a URL-encoded value is visible in whichever form it was encoded from
- 2026-07-30 · process · eleven adversarial rounds on one PR, 31 real defects, zero false positives — the privacy control alone accounted for defects in eight distinct dimensions (whole-string spelling, mixed percent encoding, numeric entities, semicolon-less entities, named entities, node kinds, zero-width normalisation, Unicode normalisation), which is the concrete cost of hand-rolling a redaction tool and the strongest argument in the record for treating "is there a library for this" as a first question rather than a fallback
- 2026-07-30 · risk · (Codex round 12, PR #26, merge-blocking) the fixture redaction control is a BLACKLIST and therefore structurally incomplete — it verifies only identities already in the substitution map plus narrow source-specific detectors, so an unlisted identity of a kind the map was never about (the reviewer's example: a `mailto:` link) is neither replaced nor rejected and is written to a public fixture with the tool reporting success; an independent reviewer judged documenting the residual risk INSUFFICIENT and called for inverting the trust boundary (extract only parser-required subtrees under an explicit allow-list, or use a mature sanitisation library with a closed output policy) before further public fixture capture
- 2026-07-30 · fact · the seven fixtures committed on PR #26 were swept empirically for the unlisted classes and are clean — no email, mailto, tel, phone number or street address in any of them, and every five-digit run is an SVG path coordinate or a TennisRecord match id rather than a postal code; the blocker is about what the capture TOOL could publish next, not about what this PR ships
- 2026-07-30 · process · asking a reviewer to rule on a DESIGN judgement, separately from "is anything blocking", produced the most valuable single answer of twelve rounds — it converted my own "documented residual risk is an acceptable trade" into an explicit external "no, that class of fail-open cannot be documented away", which is exactly the call an author is worst placed to make about their own work
- 2026-07-30 · fact · (issue #28) the allow-list policy's per-source vocabulary was seeded by re-running the EXISTING `tennisrecord`/`usta` detector regex sets from `tools/capture-fixture.ts` over the seven already-committed, already-redacted fixtures via `tools/bootstrap-vocabulary.ts` — 196 distinct skeletons for tennisrecord (139 auto-written, 57 name-shaped) and 686 for usta (419 auto-written, 267 name-shaped); the synthetic stand-in list (`tools/fixture-vocabulary/stand-ins.txt`) was seeded the same way, reusing proven machinery rather than inventing a new derivation (deviation D1 from the posted plan)
- 2026-07-30 · risk · (issue #28) the name-shape heuristic ("two or more consecutive capitalised words") is deliberately broad and over-triggers hard on nav-chrome-heavy content: 267 of USTA's 686 skeletons were flagged name-shaped, almost all of it ALL-CAPS menu labels ("SIGN IN", "US OPEN") and cookie-banner boilerplate rather than anything resembling a real name — a client-rendered SPA capture will make this manual-review set large every time, which is a real scaling cost of the allow-list design worth knowing going in, not a defect in this PR
- 2026-07-30 · risk · (issue #28) `stand-ins.txt`'s seed is NOT exhaustive of every synthetic value the fixtures contain — it only captures what the narrow `playername=`/`teamname=`/`uaid`/`tennis-id`/displayed-name/displayed-location detector patterns match. Synthetic club and city/state names embedded elsewhere on the page (e.g. `Clayview Country Club`, `Cedar Falls MO`) fell outside those patterns and surfaced as name-shaped vocabulary entries needing individual hand justification instead of being pre-elided as stand-ins; a future re-capture that introduces a new synthetic place/club name in a new markup position will hit the same gap
- 2026-07-30 · process · (issue #28) a circular import between `tools/redact-fixture.ts` and `tools/fixture-policy.ts` (each needs functions from the other: `redact()` calls the new `assertAllowListed`, `fixture-policy.ts` reuses the existing normalisation helpers) is safe under Node's ESM loader ONLY when every cross-module reference is used inside a function BODY; a `class PolicyError extends RedactionError` reference is evaluated at module TOP LEVEL and threw `Class extends value undefined` regardless of which side of the cycle loaded first — `PolicyError` was made its own `Error` subclass rather than extending `RedactionError` to avoid this, a distinction worth knowing before reaching for "just extend the sibling error class" across a deliberately circular pair of modules
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28 reconciliation) 14 name-shaped vocabulary entries across tennisrecord.txt/usta.txt carried a `# reviewed:` justification claiming "already-redacted synthetic ... it is the same class of invented value" that no code verified and no line of `stand-ins.txt` backed — several (Leawood KS, Lawrence KS, Clayview Country Club, Hallbrook Country Club) are recognisably real Kansas City-area places, meaning a future capture containing a real city/club name could have been admitted on the strength of the same unverifiable prose, exactly what `rules/testing.md` calls a check that certifies an invariant it cannot see; fixed by adding a machine-checked `# reviewed[synthetic]: <reason>` marker (`loadVocabulary()` now refuses to load a synthetic-classed entry unless every capitalised token is present in `stand-ins.txt`) and reclassifying all 14 as real-public — a category the vocabulary deliberately admits, since an organisation or place name doesn't identify an individual — with honest, entry-specific reasons; caught by reconciliation against the allow-list's own stand-in register, not by any of the 225 passing tests, since they exercised the parsing mechanism rather than the truth of the human's claim
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 self-review finding 1, critical) the DEFAULT invocation of `tools/capture-fixture.ts` (no `--detectors` flag, the documented shape) resolved `detectorSet` to `"none"`, whose `defaultVocabularyPath` returns `undefined`, and `redact()` only runs `assertAllowListed` when `options.vocabulary !== undefined` — so the most ordinary invocation of the capture tool ran with NO allow-list enforced at all and reported success, the exact fail-open shape issue #28's whole allow-list redesign exists to eliminate, surviving a full green run plus a prior reconciliation pass; fixed by having `main()` refuse outright (no bypass flag) whenever no vocabulary path resolves, naming `--vocabulary` in the error — a control that certifies an invariant only on some of its invocation paths is not a control, it is a false sense of one, and the gap didn't show up in any test because every existing test happened to pass `--vocabulary` explicitly
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 self-review finding 2) `tools/fixture-policy.ts`'s `extractAtoms` had a guard `if (node.type === "tag") { if (node.tagName === "script" || node.tagName === "style") return; ... }` that could never fire — domhandler types a `<script>`/`<style>` element's `node.type` as `"script"`/`"style"`, never `"tag"` — so those elements fell through to the generic child-walk, atomising their (already-redaction-stripped) text body while never creating an atom for their attributes at all, a publication surface entirely outside the allow-list; caught only by constructing the exact failing input directly against `extractAtoms`, since none of the seven committed fixtures' script/style tags carry attributes (redaction already strips the whole tag pair) — a reminder that "the committed fixtures all pass" does not mean "the function is correct," only that today's inputs don't exercise the gap
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-1 adversarial review finding 1, high) `redactHtml`'s `SCRIPT_OR_STYLE` regex (`/<(script|style)\b[^>]*>[\s\S]*?<\/\1>/gi`) only strips PAIRED script/style tags, and `extractAtoms` unconditionally skipped script/style CHILDREN on the assumption that redaction had already emptied them — so a truncated/malformed capture like `<script>Patrick Turner` (no closing tag) never matched the strip regex, its body survived untouched, and the skip then discarded it unread: no atom, no allow-list check, at the exact same finding-2-fixed code path. Fixed by atomising script/style TEXT children like any other node instead of skipping them — free for well-formed input (an already-stripped `<script></script>` has no children left) and closing for malformed input
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-1 adversarial review finding 2, high) `isStructuralAttrName` exempted `class`/`id`/`style`/every `aria-*`/every `data-v-*` from atomisation by NAME alone, so `aria-label="Patrick Turner"` (or the same in `id`/`class`) survived redaction with no atom, no vocabulary check, and no never-publish-class match — an uninspected PII channel, ARIA labels being content exposed to assistive technology and `id`/`class`/`style` being author-chosen free-form strings, not fixed vocabularies. Fixed by narrowing the exemption to a principled line — numeric/geometric/HTML-SVG-spec-enumerated attribute values only (`d`, `viewBox`, `width`, `role`, `type`, …) — never a free-form string; the seven committed fixtures needed +808 vocabulary entries as a result (95 tennisrecord, 713 usta; only 56 name-shaped, the rest boilerplate CSS-class/id/AEM-component tokens auto-admissible under the existing non-name-shaped convention)
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-1 adversarial review finding 3, high) `loadVocabulary`'s `# reviewed[synthetic]:` check verified each capitalised TOKEN in an entry independently against tokens drawn from `stand-ins.txt`, rather than requiring the whole entry to be accounted for by full stand-in PHRASES — since the committed register has "Avery Ashby" and "Arden Ashcroft" as separate entries, a spliced entry "Avery Ashcroft" (a full name in NEITHER stand-in) passed as machine-verified "synthetic" because "Avery" and "Ashcroft" each matched a token from a DIFFERENT stand-in. Fixed by reusing `computeSkeleton` itself (the same reduction the allow-list check runs) — an entry passes iff `computeSkeleton(entry, standIns) === ""`, eliding full phrases at word boundaries, not tokens independently
- 2026-07-30 · process · (issue #28, PR #29 round-1 adversarial review) across five structural escapes found in this control over two review rounds, EVERY one was a category of content the policy never atomised at all (an unclosed script/style body, a whole class of attribute names, an independently-token-checked synthetic marker) — never a case where the reduction ladder ran on an atom and judged its skeleton wrongly; the ladder's logic held under every attack, its INPUT SET did not. A completeness argument for this shape of control needs to enumerate every node/attribute/marker category the extractor could see and show each is atomised or explain why not, rather than reasoning forward from the categories someone already thought to add
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-2 adversarial review finding R2-1, high) `xmlns`/`fill`/`stroke` were exempted from atomisation by NAME alone even though their values are not closed enumerations — `<svg xmlns="https://example.test/Patrick-Turner">` and `fill="url(https://example.test/Patrick-Turner)"` created no atom at all, so an empty vocabulary admitted an identity written straight into a public fixture. Fixed by exempting these three names only when the VALUE matches a genuinely closed grammar (a hex colour, a CSS Level 4 named colour, or `none`/`currentColor`/`transparent`/`context-fill`/`context-stroke` for `fill`/`stroke`; one of five standard XML/SVG namespace URIs for `xmlns` — and `xlink`, since htmlparser2's HTML tokenizer reports `xmlns:xlink="…"` as a bare `xlink` attribute, confirmed empirically rather than assumed) — anything else, including a `url(...)` paint reference, is atomised like any other value
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-2 adversarial review finding R2-2, high) element names and attribute NAMES were an entirely zero-atom channel — `<patrick-turner></patrick-turner>` produced no atom of any kind, and `<div data-patrick-turner>` produced only an empty-valued attribute atom (the NAME itself was never inspected) — so a custom element or attribute name was a free identity-smuggling channel with no vocabulary check at all. Fixed by atomising the element name and every non-standard attribute name against closed, spec-derived allow-lists (`STANDARD_ELEMENT_NAMES`/`STANDARD_ATTRIBUTE_NAMES`, built from the HTML/SVG/ARIA specs, deliberately NOT derived from the fixtures); `data-*` stays a genuine open PREFIX (only the remainder after the prefix is atomised, e.g. `data-patrick-turner` → `patrick-turner`), while `aria-*` is enumerated as a CLOSED list rather than treated as a prefix, since unlike `data-*` the WAI-ARIA spec does not allow arbitrary `aria-*` suffixes
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-2 adversarial review finding R2-3, high) `isNameShaped` matched only ASCII-capitalised word pairs (`[A-Z][A-Za-z'-]*` repeated), so `patrick turner` — lowercase, the common shape of a URL slug or lower-cased page text — and any non-ASCII name (`josé garcía`) loaded from a vocabulary file with NO preceding `# reviewed:` line and were auto-writable by `bootstrapVocabulary`, defeating the human-review boundary the entire allow-list design rests on: capitalisation is not a safety boundary, and treating it as one meant the control's coverage silently depended on how a page happened to render a name. Fixed by rebuilding the test on Unicode `\p{L}`/`\p{M}` classes (any two-or-more-token alphabetic skeleton, any case or script triggers review) and renaming it `requiresReview` to describe what it now means
- 2026-07-30 · process · (issue #28, PR #29 round-2 reconciliation) across ALL EIGHT structural escapes found against this control over two adversarial-review rounds, every one was the identical SHAPE of bug — a category of document content `extractAtoms` never produced an atom for at all (an unclosed script/style body, a whole class of attribute NAMES, element NAMES, a name-only-exempted attribute value with no closed grammar, a case-sensitive review trigger) — never a rule that ran on an atom and judged its skeleton wrongly. A hand-enumerated "list of the content classes I check" is structurally a blacklist no matter how principled each individual rule is, which is the exact complaint issue #28 opened against the ORIGINAL blacklist-based redaction tool; the fix this round inverts the walk itself to be a POSITIVE accounting of every node/name/value the parser can produce (walk every domhandler node kind; atomise every element/attribute name against a closed allow-list; exempt an attribute value only by closed grammar, never by name alone) so an unrecognised future input class refuses BY CONSTRUCTION rather than shipping silently until a ninth reviewer happens to name it
- 2026-07-30 · fact · (issue #28, PR #29 round-2 reconciliation) the whole-document-accounting rewrite (element-name/attribute-name atomisation, plus dropping capitalisation as `requiresReview`'s trigger) grew the two committed vocabularies from 291/1399 real entries to 293/1449 (tennisrecord/usta) — a modest net entry count, since the new element-name/attribute-name/attribute-value channels rarely fire against already-redacted, mostly-standard markup — but it reclassified roughly 1,000 previously bare (no-justification-needed) entries as requiring a `# reviewed:` line, because `requiresReview` no longer distinguishes `Patrick Turner`-shaped content from lowercase AEM/CSS component class names and cookie-consent legal paragraphs that merely happen to be multiple words; nearly all of that churn was mechanically categorisable into the file's existing justification conventions (AEM/BEM component class names, cookie-consent boilerplate, URL/page-route path segments, opaque analytics tokens), with zero of the newly-introduced entries matching the OLD capitalised-name shape — i.e. no new capitalisation-shaped risk was hiding in the churn, only previously-invisible lowercase/mixed-case boilerplate now made to show its justification
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-3 adversarial review finding R3-1, high) `redactHtml` writes the RAW string, but `assertAllowListed`/`extractAtoms` inspected only cheerio's RECOVERED DOM — wherever the parser recovers from malformed markup (an orphan end tag like `</Patrick Turner>`, discarded outright by the HTML5 tree builder), those are two different documents and only one was checked, so `redact('<div></Patrick Turner>', [], { vocabulary: new Set() })` returned the identity-bearing raw string with zero atoms and a passing check. A checker that validates a PARSED representation while a RAW string is what gets PUBLISHED is checking a different document than the one it publishes — parse-recovery divergence is a bypass class in its own right, distinct from "a content category the walk never atomised" (the shape of all eight prior findings). Fixed by loading with `sourceCodeLocationInfo: true` and separately accounting for every byte the parser discarded: the complement of every kept node's source range catches an orphan token BETWEEN two real nodes, and a text-node-scoped scan (cross-checked against every other node's real start/end-tag location, to avoid flagging HTML5's "after `</body>`, trailing whitespace merges into body's last text node" shape) catches an orphan token the tree builder merged INTO a text node's reported span without it appearing in that node's `.data`. Zero vocabulary entries were needed for the seven committed fixtures.
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (issue #28, PR #29 round-3 adversarial review finding R3-2, high) round 2 (finding R2-1) established that name-only attribute-value exemption is unsafe as a PRINCIPLE and fixed it for exactly the three attributes that round happened to name (`xmlns`/`fill`/`stroke`) — but left every OTHER "structural" attribute (`d`, `viewBox`, `width`, `height`, `colspan`, `rowspan`, `transform`, `role`, `type`, `points`, `preserveAspectRatio`, `focusable`) exempt by name alone in a separate `NAME_ONLY_STRUCTURAL_ATTR_NAMES` set, so `<path d="Patrick Turner">`/`<img width="Patrick Turner">`/`<div role="Patrick Turner">` all produced zero attribute atoms with an empty vocabulary — the identical mistake reproduced one round later because the FIX was scoped to the instances the reviewer named rather than to the principle the reviewer stated. Applying a review's lesson only to the specific instances it named, rather than to the principle it stated, reproduced the same defect class one round later. Fixed by retiring `NAME_ONLY_STRUCTURAL_ATTR_NAMES` entirely and giving every structural attribute its own closed value grammar (SVG path/point-data command grammar, an exactly-4-number `viewBox`, a numeric-with-optional-unit `width`/`height`, integer `colspan`/`rowspan`, a closed transform-function grammar, a closed ARIA role set, a closed `type` token set, closed `preserveAspectRatio`/`focusable` token sets) — a value outside its grammar is atomised, not silently admitted. One vocabulary entry was needed across both fixtures: `imgage`, a real (non-ARIA) `role` value from a third-party Google Custom Search widget's inline SVG — an apparent upstream typo in vendor markup, not personal data.
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (CodeQL js/redos, high, PR #29) the closed `transform` attribute grammar added to close a round-3 finding was itself exponential — `\s*[,\s]\s*` inside a `*` group can consume one whitespace run many ways, so `scale(0` plus repeated `\t\t0` backtracks catastrophically; replaced with a linear grammar walk (`[^()]*` chunks + a plain split) per `rules/scripting.md`'s implement-the-grammar rule, with a regression test that asserts a time bound rather than only a verdict, because the failure mode of a returning ReDoS is a hang rather than a wrong answer
- 2026-07-30 · process · closing a privacy finding with a hand-written regex grammar introduced a high-severity security alert in the same commit — a required check caught it, but the general lesson is that a regex written to make a control STRICTER is still new attack surface and deserves the same scrutiny as the hole it closes
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#15) drizzle-orm's `onConflictDoNothing({ target, where })` on better-sqlite3 emits the partial-index `WHERE` clause AFTER `do nothing` (`on conflict (cols) do nothing where …`), which SQLite rejects outright — a targeted partial-index upsert has to use `onConflictDoUpdate` instead, whose `targetWhere` is placed correctly (before `do update set`), even when there is nothing meaningful to update (e.g. `team_memberships`, whose `set` re-assigns a key column to itself as a harmless no-op); confirmed empirically against a real `SqliteError`, not assumed from the drizzle docs
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#15) drizzle-orm's `db.transaction(cb)` callback argument (`SQLiteTransaction`) and the top-level `db` from `openDb()` (`BetterSQLite3Database & { $client }`) are NOT the same type — a shared upsert/identity module typed against the concrete `BetterSQLite3Database` fails to typecheck when called with the transaction handle passed by `db.transaction`; typing every write function against `BaseSQLiteDatabase<'sync', RunResult>` (the common ancestor of both) fixes it and is what actually lets "one transaction per pull" (spec § Ingestion) share the same write functions inside and outside a transaction
- 2026-07-30 · fact · (#15) TennisRecord's team page's local-schedule table (`div.large`, second occurrence, anchored on its own "Local Schedule" header cell rather than positionally) carries `team_matches` candidates with a `mid=` source match id, but no per-player detail — corroborating the Phase-2 note that it's a cheaper `team_matches` path than TennisLink; the roster table's per-row `profile.aspx?playername=…` links are the team→player cascade seam `tn team pull --players` walks
- 2026-07-30 · idea · (#15) `tn team pull`/`tn player pull` resolve every roster/opponent/partner name through the same fuzzy identity ladder and abort the WHOLE pull's transaction on any ambiguous match rather than writing a partial roster — simple and safe for v1, but a scouting-heavy team page could plausibly hit a real ambiguity often enough that a partial-write-with-a-warning mode (skip just the ambiguous row, like the existing no-profile-link skip) is worth revisiting once real usage shows how often it fires
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, self-review) a team page's UNPLAYED fixtures have no result link and therefore no `mid=`, so their `team_matches` rows took `upsertTeamMatch`'s plain-insert branch and re-pulling a mid-season page grew one duplicate row per unplayed fixture PER PULL — the headline idempotency promise, falsified by exactly the rows the committed fixture (an end-of-season page where all 10 rows have results) never contained; fixed by deduping an id-less row on its natural composite (home team, visiting team, date), and the same landmine was defused in `upsertCourtMatch` before a future source could step on it
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, self-review) `hrefParam` returns `""` for a bare `mid=`, and `""` is NOT NULL to SQLite, so every empty-id schedule row would have collided into a single `team_matches` row under the partial unique index — the exact argument `match-history.ts` already carries from Codex round 7 on PR #26 ("an empty idempotency key is as unusable as a missing one"), which the schedule parser had not inherited; applying a review's stated PRINCIPLE only to the instances it NAMED is the #28 lesson repeating one PR later
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, self-review) `upsertTeam`'s conflict `set` wrote `values.x ?? null` for every optional column, so a TennisRecord pull — which knows nothing about `tennislink_url` and passes `district: null` unconditionally — silently erased whatever another source had recorded, on every re-pull; latent only because nothing sets those columns yet, and #27 (TennisLink) is the run that would have hit it
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, self-review) the archive path guard constrained only the LEAF ("is the file under rawRoot?"), which a misconfigured `TN_RAW_PATH=src` satisfies trivially while writing un-redacted captures into a TRACKED directory of a PUBLIC repo — the plan specified "inside the repo tree except under raw/", so the root itself must be constrained; the same shape as #28's ten defects, where the rule was right and the INPUT it was applied to was wrong
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#15, self-review) three pull tests never set `TN_RAW_PATH`, so `npm test` wrote captured pages into the repo's own `raw/` on every run — harmless today because `raw/` is gitignored, but the PR checklist asserted "archive tests only ever write into a temp TN_RAW_PATH", which was false; a claim in a checklist is not evidence, and this one was checkable in one grep
- 2026-07-30 · note · (#15) `home`/`visiting` on `team_matches` and `side` on `court_match_players` encode the PERSPECTIVE THE PAGE WAS PULLED FROM, not real home/away — TennisRecord does not state which team hosted. Pulling two opposing teams rewrites the labels to the later pull's perspective; every column flips together so the row stays self-consistent and semantically equivalent, but the labels are NOT stable across pull order and Phase 4 must not read them as real home/away
- 2026-07-30 · note · (#15) `upsertCourtMatchPlayers` never deletes rows, so if a source page is corrected to name different opponents, the superseded participants remain attached to the court match — a re-pull adds but never retracts; low likelihood, but a dossier reading participant counts should not assume exactly 2 or 4
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31, rated critical) a LEXICAL path check answers "what does this string say", not "where do the bytes land" — `resolve()` never consults the disk, so making `<repo>/raw` a symlink to `<repo>/src` (or pointing `TN_RAW_PATH` at an external symlink that targets the repo) satisfied every string comparison in the privacy guard while `writeFileSync` followed the link into tracked source; fixed by re-validating the root at its REAL location (via the nearest EXISTING ancestor, since the root legitimately does not exist before the first capture), refusing symlinked components inside the root, re-checking after `mkdirSync` — which treats an existing symlink-to-a-directory as "already there" — and writing with `flag: "wx"` so the leaf cannot be followed either
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31, rated high) the fix for the id-less-fixture duplicate REPRODUCED THE DEFECT ONE PERSPECTIVE OVER: it deduped on ORDERED `(home, visiting, date)`, but team-pull assigns the PULLED team to `home`, so the same unplayed fixture pulled from the opposing team's page searched `(B,A,date)`, missed `(A,B,date)` and inserted a duplicate anyway; the key has to be the UNORDERED team pair, and court counts arriving from the opposite orientation are swapped into the stored row's orientation rather than written straight through — a fix that is correct only for the instance that motivated it is the #28 lesson yet again
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31, rated high) `tn team pull --players` committed the team transaction BEFORE the cascade and then only warned on each failed player pull, so the command returned `ok` with exit 0 having enriched ZERO of the players it was explicitly asked to enrich, with an irreversible team write already on disk; a requested-but-unfulfilled cascade now reports `status=partial`, names the entries, and exits non-zero — the outcome has to be visible to a caller that reads only the exit code
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31, rated medium) one identity ladder cannot run two notions of "the same name": tier 2 folded case in SQLite (`lower()`, ASCII-only) while the canonical-name half of the SAME tier folded in JS (Unicode), so an alias `Élodie` never matched a source spelling `élodie` and a second row was created for someone already on file; both halves now fold in JS, and the read-back-after-insert (which shared the ASCII-only `lower()`) is replaced by `.returning()`
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31, rated medium) a read-sleep-write politeness delay is not serialized — `Promise.all` had every caller observe the same "last call" timestamp, and the third saw the interval already elapsed and skipped its wait entirely, so requests went out together exactly when pacing matters most; each caller now claims its slot SYNCHRONOUSLY before its first await
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31, rated medium) `response.text()` ran BEFORE the `response.ok` check, so a server that sent 404/500 headers and then streamed an endless body blocked the caller to the abort timeout and surfaced a timeout instead of the documented `FetchError` — the existing tests used only finite error bodies, so they structurally could not observe it
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#15) a fix written under review pressure needs its own test run before it is believed: the realpath hardening threw ENOENT on a raw root that did not exist yet — i.e. every first capture on a fresh machine — and it was the newly-written regression test, not the reasoning, that caught it
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31 round 2, rated high) the unordered-pair fix for id-less fixtures OVER-merged: date alone is not an identity, so a same-day doubleheader between the same two teams (09:00 and 17:00, neither with a `mid=`) collapsed into one row and the second was lost undetectably — and the schedule parser was READING the Time and Match Site columns (both are in its column contract) while the emitted row discarded them; persisted `team_matches.scheduled_time` + `site` and added them to the id-less key. Two rounds on the same three lines, each fix correct for the case that motivated it and wrong one case over — the argument for a discriminator you can point at, rather than one you reason your way to
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31 round 2, rated medium) a politeness reservation records when a request was MEANT to go out, not when it did — an event-loop stall past the reserved slot let a later caller reserve from the stale intended time and start too soon after it, violating the interval exactly under load; the slot is now re-anchored (monotonically) to the actual wake time
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31 round 2, rated medium) throwing `FetchError` before reading a non-2xx body fixed the blocking-until-timeout defect but left the response stream and its socket alive until the abort fired 20s later, so a server streaming an endless error body accumulated one live stream per call; the body is now explicitly cancelled (best-effort, so a locked/errored body cannot mask the FetchError)
- 2026-07-30 · note · (#15) `archivePage` writes through the directory's REALPATH and validates where it points, rather than only rejecting components that ARE symlinks — "is this a link?" is a weaker question than "where does it lead?". A check-then-use window remains and is NOT claimed closed: pure Node has no `openat`/`O_NOFOLLOW` directory-handle write, and a native helper is a dependency the plan forbids. Escalated to the HC rather than silently accepted
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31 round 3, rated high — CRITICAL by PROJECT.md's framework, since it ships broken) the realpath hardening applied the ROOT-ONLY allowlist to a DESCENDANT directory, so with `TN_RAW_PATH` unset — the documented default — `<repo>/raw/tennisrecord` was rejected and EVERY pull threw before writing a byte; not one test caught it because every test set `TN_RAW_PATH` to a temp dir, so the suite exercised only the configuration it had invented and never the one the docs describe. A privacy control that refuses everything looks identical to a privacy control that works, right up until someone runs it
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31 round 3, rated high) the id-less fixture key compared `scheduled_time` and `site` as EXACT strings, so the same fixture re-rendered as `09:00 AM` rather than `9:00 AM` missed its stored row and duplicated — format sensitivity in the one key that exists precisely because there is no `mid=` to fall back on; the SQL now narrows on the indexable, stable parts (both teams, the date, no source id) and a normalized comparison picks the match out of that small candidate set, with the DISPLAY values still stored
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#15) three independent review rounds landed on the SAME three lines of `upsertTeamMatch`, and each fix was correct for the case that motivated it and wrong one case over: ordered pair → unordered pair → unordered pair + date → + time/site → + normalized time/site. The lesson is not "think harder" — it is that a key assembled by reasoning about cases keeps inheriting the case you last thought about, and only an adversary enumerating the input space finds the next one
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#15, Codex adversarial review PR #31 round 4, rated high) a normalizer that can map a NON-NULL input onto the NULL key merges two facts that are not the same fact: stripping non-alphanumerics turned `"—"` (how a schedule renders a time not yet set) into `""`, exactly the key `null` used, so a dash-timed fixture matched a fixture with no time at all and one real row was silently lost; fixed with disjoint TAGGED key domains — `" null"`, `clock:HH:MM`, `raw:<stripped>`, and `lit:<original>` when stripping would leave nothing — so no non-null input can produce the null key and two different punctuation-only values stay distinct from each other
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#15) FOUR review rounds landed on `upsertTeamMatch`'s id-less key, and the last two were defects introduced BY THE PREVIOUS ROUND'S FIX — a broken default root, then a key collision in the normalizer written to fix a key collision. The through-line is that each fix was validated against the case that motivated it and never against the space around it; the counter-practice that actually worked was reproducing the defect first (10→11 rows, ArchivePathError under the documented default) and writing the test before the fix, because both times the test found something the reasoning had not
- 2026-07-30 · process · nadal's kickoff rule "every SOW includes a manual-test segment for HCs plus runbooks for manual testing" is encoded in NO artifact: `skills/final/SKILL.md`'s SOW template carries only *Testing Coverage* (automated coverage + Quality Checks results), no stage in `skills/` or `docs/standards/development-lifecycle.md` requires an HC segment, and the sole trace anywhere is an orphan assertion in `docs/runbooks/README.md` ("each SOW's manual-test segment cites the runbook it exercises") describing a thing nothing produces — so #15 shipped `tn team pull`/`tn player pull` with zero HC test instructions and the HC had to ask unprompted whether anything needed manual testing, while `pre-tournament-full-pull.md`, the runbook covering the exact commands that PR shipped, stayed listed as *Planned*. A rule held only in conversation and agent memory does not survive a session boundary; the durable form is the stage template that emits the artifact
- 2026-07-30 · process · (converted from issue #34, closed — an Issue must not spawn from this log) the Rules-Layer dispositions proposed off #28+#31's 22 same-shape findings, recorded here for triage rather than as tracked work. `rules/testing.md` → Anti-Patterns: *testing only the configuration the test invented* (every archive test set `TN_RAW_PATH` to a temp dir, so nothing exercised the documented default and the feature shipped throwing on every call under it — a component that reads config needs at least one test at the DEFAULT value); *asserting a fixture's incidental shape as if it were the domain* (the committed team fixture is an end-of-season page where every row has a result link, which is exactly why two idempotency defects stayed invisible — when a fixture cannot exhibit a failure mode, construct the input that can); *fixing the named instance instead of the class*. `rules/backend.md` → Patterns: *a normalizer must not be able to map a non-null input onto the null key* (use disjoint tagged domains when one key spans several value shapes); *an identity key assembled by reasoning about cases inherits the last case you thought about*. `rules/self-review.md`: *reproduce before fixing* — a fix whose defect was never reproduced is a hypothesis. Disposition is triage's call: these are ace rules, so do-now is not available; upstream-to-ace or drop

PR #31 hit exactly this at `b333c8f` after five rounds and correctly deferred rather than folding — the gate working as designed, but the fold window is a scheduling constraint no stage states out loud
- 2026-07-30 · process · (#16) `PROJECT.md` → *Rule-suggestion disposition* ships ace's `autonomous-fold`, which instructs `final` to defer large or contentious Rules-Layer suggestions **to a follow-up issue recorded in the SOW** — the exact thing this log's own header forbids (*"No Issues, PRs, rules, or ADRs spawn directly from this log"*). Issue #34 is that instruction being followed, and it was closed on 2026-07-31 as never-should-have-existed. A vendored default and a project rule disagreed, the vendored one was nearer to hand at the moment of decision, and it won. Until the two agree this recurs on every run whose `final` learns a rules lesson it cannot fold. The line nadal actually draws: rules findings / process recommendations / ace-side improvements → this log; product scope deferred from a SOW, and defects → tracked issues (#27, #30, #32, #33 are legitimate on that footing). Deliberately NOT fixed in-flight: using `autonomous-fold` to justify folding a fix to `autonomous-fold` is circular, and this log's whole point is that the HC triages

Knowing the pattern did not prevent it. The three sharpest instances: a collision fix that covered the batch path and not the single-team path (the "named instance, not the class" shape, now four occurrences); a collision scheme whose determinism rested on DB-id ordering that NEITHER query requested, with tests that passed only because SQLite happened to return insertion order; and a locale pin whose regression test was itself a false green that would pass on any machine without a French message catalog. A fail-open security control whose error path is indistinguishable from the guarded state is not a control; the three-way tracked/untracked/indeterminate result now fails CLOSED on indeterminate
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#16, self-review after Codex round 2, rated critical) git TRANSLATES "did not match any file(s) known to git" under NLS, so the English string match that decided "untracked" failed under a French locale, resolved to indeterminate, and — because the new code correctly failed closed — refused EVERY capture and EVERY dossier write on any machine not running in English. All 628 tests passed, because the test machine is English. Byte-for-byte the PR #31 round-3 defect: a privacy control that refuses everything looks identical to one that works, right up until someone runs it. Locale now pinned (LC_ALL=C, LANGUAGE cleared, since LANGUAGE overrides LC_ALL for message translation specifically)

- 2026-07-30 · process · (#16) rounds 1–3 ran the reviewer unscoped and it drifted into hardening the filesystem guard against an adversary strictly MORE capable than the one #33 already accepts as out of scope for v1 — real findings, but ones the HC had already dispositioned by accepting #33. From round 4 the reviewer was asked to split findings into "reachable without an adversary" and "inside #33's accepted boundary", and the rounds converged immediately (1 finding, then 0). An adversarial reviewer with no stated threat model will keep finding true things forever; the threat model is an input to the review, not a conclusion from it
- 2026-07-30 · risk · (converted from issue #30, closed — a review-residual register belongs in this log, not in a tracked issue) the fixture allow-list does not constrain NUMERIC identifiers: `computeSkeleton("8165551234", [])` is `""`, because the structural class elides bare digit runs of any length, so a numeric identifier reduces to an empty skeleton and is admitted unconditionally. Four entries (`Cedar Falls MO`, `Halden CA`, `Norwood MO`, `Wexley KS`) sit in that class on the conservative default for indeterminate place-shaped content, and the claim each one makes is "this is structurally a place name, not a person's name" — never "this municipality is confirmed real"

- 2026-07-30 · note · (#17, PR A) spec § Stack names "Hono for the MCP server" — wrong for what v1 actually needs. Hono is an HTTP framework, required only for MCP's streamable-HTTP transport; the consumer is a local agent chat on Randy's own laptop talking to a local SQLite file, and stdio needs no port, bind address, or auth story at all. `tn mcp serve` speaks stdio; nothing under `src/` imports Hono. Same shape as #36's Playwright finding — a spec-stated dependency corrected in the open rather than silently. Caveat worth knowing: `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` itself still pulls `@hono/node-server` in transitively (an unconditional dependency of the whole package, not gated on which transport a caller actually uses), so it lands in `node_modules`/`package-lock.json` regardless — that is the SDK's supply chain, not a choice this PR made

The moment it went live, a real rendered dossier printed `vs player #1` in the "prior meetings vs our players" section — exactly the deferred tradeoff becoming a live, embarrassing defect the instant its precondition (a designated home team) is met. Fixed by resolving every `versusTeamId` roster player's canonical name once in `getTeamProfile` (the DB-access assembly layer, not `derive.ts`'s pure `headToHead`) and carrying it on `TeamCrossHeadToHead.opponentName`; both renderers now print the name, escaped through each format's own existing escaper (`escapeHtml`/`escapeMarkdownCell`) rather than a new one
- 2026-07-30 · bug · (#17, PR A, self-review — caught by actually running the built `tn mcp serve` and piping a finite script into it, not by the suite) `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`'s `StdioServerTransport` never calls its own `onclose` on stdin EOF — it only attaches `'data'`/`'error'` listeners to stdin (confirmed by reading `server/stdio.js` directly), `onclose` fires ONLY on an explicit `close()`. `src/cli/commands/mcp-serve.ts`'s runner resolved its `Promise<number>` solely on `transport.onclose`, so piping a finite JSON-RPC script into a real `tn mcp serve` and letting stdin hit EOF left the promise unsettled forever — Node exited with **code 13** ("unsettled top-level await") and a stderr warning instead of a clean 0, on every graceful (non-killed) shutdown. The SDK's own type signature gives no hint of this: `Transport.close()` is documented as closing the connection, but nothing states stdin EOF triggers it. Fixed by having the runner listen for stdin EOF itself and call `transport.close()` (which DOES fire `onclose`, so that half of the contract holds) — `stdin` is injected (defaulting to `process.stdin`) so the fix has a direct unit test rather than only the manual reproduction that found it
- 2026-07-31 · bug · (#17, PR A, Stage-4 verify, rated high) `setAvailability` validated `status` with zod and left `day` — the OTHER half of the `(player_id, event_id, day)` upsert key — an unvalidated `z.string().min(1)` on both surfaces. `eventsForDay` compares dates as TEXT, so any malformed day that merely SORTED inside an event's range matched that event and was persisted verbatim: `"2026-08-28 "`, `"2026-08-28xyz"` and `"2026-08-2900"` each wrote a SEPARATE row beside the real `"2026-08-28"` — four rows for one day, defeating the idempotent upsert the unique index exists to provide and inventing days no per-event-day read (lineup planning, PR B) will ever match. Byte-for-byte the `upsertTeamMatch` shape logged four times above: a key assembled without normalizing its input domain, where the sibling field got a validator and the key field did not. The plan's own testing strategy enumerated "unknown status rejected" and never "malformed day rejected" — the gap was in the specification of what to assert, not in the implementation of it, which is the #16 lesson one phase on. Reproduced against a real DB (4 rows) before any fix, closed at the write service so both presenters inherit it, and the guard is a real calendar-date check rather than a pattern (`"2026-02-31"` matches the pattern and is not a date; JS would silently roll it forward, which is the same quiet substitution in another costume)

The deferral is the interesting part: this repo's logged failure mode is successive structural fixes in one PR generating the next defect (PR #31, PR #38, and now #41), and the counter-move is a tracked issue, not a fourth swing. `GRAMMAR.md` was corrected to document the exception rather than keep promising behavior the code does not have — a doc that overclaims is the same failure as a check whose comment overclaims

The lesson generalizes past drizzle: a branch that adds a migration or changes a lookup path has a merge conflict that lives in BEHAVIOR, not in text, and `git merge` reports clean on exactly the parts that matter least. The counter-practice that caught it here was running the full suite plus a fresh-DB `db migrate` AFTER resolving, instead of trusting a conflict-free merge

The recommended fix — a compatibility/repair path reconciling drizzle's migration state — would mean carrying migration-reconciliation machinery in production forever to serve a window that is already closing, on a database the spec deliberately makes disposable (idempotent upserts over an archived `raw/`, i.e. a cache, not a system of record).
